## Reactions

On 30 July, the Carter Center issued a harsh rebuke of the election authorities,[^cite_note-CarterCenterBlasts-155][^cite_note-323] stating there was a "complete lack of transparency" and that the election "cannot be considered democratic".[^cite_note-CarterCenterPressRelease-156]

### Fraud allegations

See also: [§ Sequences of zeros in the CNE values](#Sequences_of_zeros_in_the_CNE_values)

[Anne Applebaum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anne_Applebaum) wrote in *[The Atlantic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Atlantic)* that it "was absolutely clear by \[31 July] that ... the election had been stolen".[^cite_note-AtlanticDictatorLies-26] Independent observers have described the election results as arbitrary, even by Venezuelan standards, according to *The Guardian*.[^cite_note-KnifeEdge-255]

[Political scientist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_scientist) [Steven Levitsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steven_Levitsky) called the vote "one of the most egregious electoral frauds in modern Latin American history."[^cite_note-Turkewitz-2024a-28]

Political scientist Dalson Figueiredo of the [Federal University of Pernambuco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_University_of_Pernambuco), one of the authors of the [AltaVista parallel vote tabulation project](#Parallel_vote_tabulation), stated: "After analyzing our own data, seeing the opposition's results and how they both match up, my conclusion is that we're witnessing the largest electoral fraud in the history of Latin America."[^cite_note-WAPOMaduroLost-24]

On his [blog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blog), [mathematician](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mathematician) [Terence Tao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terence_Tao) pointed out that the election results had "oddly round percentages", suggesting that the election was fixed.[^cite_note-324]

### Domestic



Machado (left) and González (middle) along with his wife (right), addressing the nation in front of the United Nations Development Programme office in Caracas, 2024

Nicolás Maduro called González Urrutia a "coward" during a speech on 30 July 2024: "Come for me, I am waiting for you at Miraflores, Mr. González Urrutia, don't take long to arrive, coward!".

Supporting the PUD/González win announcement, María Corina Machado and Edmundo González rejected the results from the CNE and claimed victory.[^cite_note-APLive-325] On 31 July, Maduro alleged that the US was fomenting civil war in Venezuela.[^cite_note-USMaduroLost-326] González, at an event accompanied by Machado, said "The Venezuelans and the entire world know what happened ... Our struggle continues and we will not rest until the will of the Venezuelan people is respected".[^cite_note-APLive-325]

The [Communist Party of Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_Party_of_Venezuela) objected to the CNE announcement and called for the complete election results to be published.[^cite_note-TribunaPopular_about_election-327]

Supporting the CNE's announcement of his victory, Maduro described the result as "a triumph of peace and stability".[^cite_note-328] Candidates [Benjamín Rausseo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjam%C3%ADn_Rausseo), Daniel Ceballos, Luis Eduardo Martínez and [José Brito](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Brito_\(Venezuelan_politician\)) recognized the official results;[^cite_note-329] the latter three were nominated by [intervened parties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interventions_of_political_parties_in_Venezuela).[^cite_note-da_Costa-2024-203] Although still maintaining that he won, Maduro acknowledged the unrest in Venezuela. He blamed the turmoil on "international Zionism", referencing an [antisemitic trope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitic_trope#World_domination) that Jews control the world.[^cite_note-330][^cite_note-331]

A [Meganálisis poll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polls_in_Venezuela#Credibility_of_polling_firms) of 1,007 people in Venezuela from 8 to 11 August found that 93% of respondents believe Maduro lost the election, with the majority of Venezuelans expressing anger, anxiety, indignation, and feelings of helplessness after the election. Venezuelans who responded that they were happy were 2.8%, and the poll found that 40% hoped to leave the country before the end of 2024.[^cite_note-HundredThousand-332]

### International

Main article: [International reactions to the 2024 Venezuelan presidential election](77467581.md)

| icon | This section **may incorporate text from a [large language model](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Large_language_models), which is [prohibited in Wikipedia articles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:LLM)**. It may include [hallucinated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hallucination_\(artificial_intelligence\)) information, [copyright violations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:COPYVIO), claims not [verified](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:V) in cited sources, [original research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:OR), or [fictitious references](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Fictitious_references). Any such material should be [removed](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2024_Venezuelan_presidential_election\&action=edit). *(January 2026)*_&#x20;([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Maintenance_template_removal))_ |
| --- | --- |

The election results released by the government-controlled National Electoral Council (CNE) were followed by a mixture of scepticism and criticism from the leaders of most Latin American countries. Some Latin American countries—including Cuba, Honduras, and Nicaragua—recognized and congratulated Maduro as the election winner.[^cite_note-reuters29jul-30][^cite_note-HardtoBelieve-29] Some world leaders expressed skepticism of the claimed results and did not recognize the CNE claims;[^cite_note-HardtoBelieve-29][^cite_note-WorldLeadersCast-31][^cite_note-reuters29jul-30] others including Russia, China, Iran and North Korea recognized [Nicolás Maduro](7161476.md) as the winner.[^cite_note-WorldLeadersCast-31][^cite_note-TimesFlees-32]



Map showing the countries (in blue) that have declared [Edmundo González](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmundo_Gonz%C3%A1lez) the winner of the presidential election. Venezuela in black.

President [Gabriel Boric](47329497.md) of Chile was the first foreign leader to question the CNE result, stating that the "results are difficult to believe".[^cite_note-Arroyo-2024a-333] President [Daniel Noboa](73980059.md) of Ecuador warned that "that is the danger of dictatorship, and today we are witnessing how one more of them tries to take hope away from millions of Venezuelans."[^cite_note-reuters29jul-30] Condemnation from Costa Rica, El Salvador, Peru and Uruguay described the CNE result in terms of fraud or corruption.[^cite_note-reuters29jul-30][^cite_note-HardtoBelieve-29][^cite_note-Arroyo-2024a-333] Harsh criticism came from President [Javier Milei](56599840.md) of Argentina, who called Maduro a dictator.[^cite_note-reuters29jul-30][^cite_note-APLive-325]

The three leftist presidents of Brazil, Colombia and Mexico[^cite_note-AtlanticDictatorLies-26][^cite_note-GuardianEditorial-334][^cite_note-FPMomentofTruth-295] were quick in demanding that all votes be counted, along with full transparency of all ballot records from each precinct.[^cite_note-NegotiateStatement-335][^cite_note-Barrons.com-3][^cite_note-reuters29jul-30] The Colombian government called for the "total vote count, its verification and independent audit to be carried out as soon as possible".[^cite_note-reuters29jul-30] President Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva of Brazil called the controversy a "normal" process, but asked for the release of the total vote tally.[^cite_note-lula-30-7-336] President Andrés Manuel López Obrador of Mexico said on 30 July that the vote tallies should be publicized, but he saw no evidence of fraud.[^cite_note-337] On 1 August, the three presidents released a joint statement of concern over post-election violence, and asking for "impartial verification of results"[^cite_note-FPMomentofTruth-295] quickly, at the disaggregated level.[^cite_note-BMCCall-294] Officials from the three nations—whose governments are allied with Maduro according to the Associated Press (AP)—worked to seek "a solution to the country's political crisis".[^cite_note-APNews6Aug-338] The AP wrote that the opposition has reason to be wary of recommendations from this group to "follow Venezuelan laws and appear before the appropriate institutions", since the "ruling party controls every aspect of government, including the justice system, and uses it to defeat and repress real and perceived opponents".[^cite_note-APNews6Aug-338]

United States [Secretary of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretary_of_State_\(United_States\)) [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken) initially expressed doubts about the veracity of the results from the [CNE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consejo_Nacional_Electoral_\(Venezuela\)),[^cite_note-reuters29jul-30][^cite_note-ReutersUSAccuses-339] and on 1 August, said there was "overwhelming evidence" that González won.[^cite_note-BBCOverwhelming-340] He called for talks and a peaceful transition,[^cite_note-USMaduroLost-326] but did not refer to González as *president-elect*.[^cite_note-USSupports-341][^cite_note-345]

On 29 July, Canada's deputy prime minister [Chrystia Freeland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chrystia_Freeland) expressed that Canada had "serious concerns" about the proclaimed election results. Freeland emphasized Canada's long-standing support for Venezuela's democratic opposition and condemned the increasingly authoritarian nature of the regime.[^cite_note-cbc_29jul-346] In January 2025, Canada formally recognized González as president-elect. The minister of Foreign Affairs [Mélanie Joly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%A9lanie_Joly) declared: "Maduro's shameless actions demonstrate that democracy and the rule of law cannot be taken for granted. We will not tolerate the erosion of the democratic process or the repression of citizens seeking to express their rights."[^cite_note-Guardian_Maduro_sworn_in_outrage-347]

The day after the election, nine Latin American countries (Argentina, Costa Rica, the Dominican Republic, Ecuador, Guatemala, Panama, Paraguay, Peru, and Uruguay[^cite_note-NineOAS-348][^cite_note-Arroyo-2024b-349]) called for an emergency meeting of the [Organization of American States](22788.md) (OAS), for 31 July.[^cite_note-ReutersResultsUnreliable-350][^cite_note-351] The member states did not reach consensus on a resolution.[^cite_note-352][^cite_note-353] OAS Secretary General [Luis Almagro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_Almagro)—referencing the ongoing investigation in the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) (ICC) of Venezuela for crimes against humanity—said he would petition the ICC for the arrest of Maduro. Prior to the election, Maduro had stated that if he did not win, there would be a "bloodbath, a civil war"; Almagro said Maduro was fulfilling that promise and it was time for justice.[^cite_note-AlmagroSolicitara-187]

The BBC and *The Guardian* reported that Peru was the first country to recognize González as Venezuela's president-elect, on 30 July[^cite_note-PeruSeConvierte-354][^cite_note-Phillips-2024-355]—a statement from the former Peruvian foreign minister that was clarified on 5 September by Peru's Council of Ministers after a new foreign minister was named.[^cite_note-356] Peru had recalled its ambassador from Venezuela on 29 July,[^cite_note-reuters29jul-30][^cite_note-APLive-325] and expelled the Venezuelan diplomats from Peru the next day.[^cite_note-357][^cite_note-358] In response, Venezuela severed diplomatic relations with Peru.[^cite_note-PeruSeConvierte-354][^cite_note-BrazilRunning-359] Peruvian President [Dina Boluarte](67870874.md) reinforced on 6 September that Peru's position with respect to Venezuela had not changed under the new foreign minister, saying "We will not be part of an electoral fraud; we will not support a dictatorial government."[^cite_note-PeruRatifies-360]

Panama suspended diplomatic relations with Venezuela.[^cite_note-PanamaSuspends-361] Venezuela also expelled diplomats from Argentina, Chile, Costa Rica, the Dominican Republic, Panama and Uruguay; as of 1 August, Brazil took over running the Caracas embassies of Argentina and Peru.[^cite_note-BrazilRunning-359][^cite_note-362]

In November 2024, the prime minister of Italy [Giorgia Meloni](29228976.md) recognized Edmundo González as the president-elect of Venezuela.[^cite_note-363] The Paraguayan government recognised González as president-elect in early January 2025.[^cite_note-GovPY_recognizes_Gonzalez-364]

## Aftermath

Main article: [2024 Venezuelan political crisis](77669383.md)

Further information: [2026 United States strikes in Venezuela](82021123.md)

Following the announcements of election results, a national and international [political crisis](77669383.md) developed.[^cite_note-FacesSetback-365][^cite_note-Kurmanaev-NYT-6-8-2024-297][^cite_note-DragsOn-366]

As of 2 September 2024, NGO [Foro Penal](54003280.md) reported that Venezuela had 1,793 political prisoners—the highest it had had in the 21st century—among which 1,659, including children, were detained after the election; most had been charged with terrorism and had not been sentenced.[^cite_note-LastHill-367][^cite_note-ElDiario1793-368]

### Protests

Main article: [2024 Venezuelan protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Venezuelan_protests)













Protests and crackdown followed the announcement of CNE results

Venezuelan citizens who considered the results to be fraudulent [took to the streets in ongoing protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Venezuelan_protests).[^cite_note-369][^cite_note-370][^cite_note-371] As of 15 August, 25 people had been killed during protests nationwide.[^cite_note-HundredThousand-332] [Amnesty International](18947898.md) and other human rights organizations "condemned the repressive actions of the Nicolás Maduro regime in Venezuela and demanded that it guarantee the right to protest and full respect for the rights to life, personal integrity and freedom".[^cite_note-InfoBaeDR-372][^cite_note-AICondemnViolence-373]

### Crackdown

Further information: [Operation Tun Tun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Tun_Tun)

Maduro accuses the opposition of promoting a coup.[^cite_note-BBCRepressionWorsens-374] In a crackdown by security forces following the elections,[^cite_note-ReportProtesters-375][^cite_note-BloombergCrackdown-376][^cite_note-MaduroBoasts-377] he mentioned using [Operation Tun Tun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Tun_Tun); [BBC News](1139893.md) stated that "rights groups say it consists of the authorities going door-to-door to detain those with links to the protests or the opposition".[^cite_note-BBCRepressionWorsens-374] Maduro ordered two prisons to be rehabilitated to contain the large number of detainees.[^cite_note-IntensifiesPersecution-378]

After [Diosdado Cabello](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diosdado_Cabello) and [Jorge Rodríguez](8767262.md) suggested that Machado and González go to prison,[^cite_note-ElPaisIrPresos-300][^cite_note-WaPo16Dead-379] Maduro asked authorities to apply "maximum justice" and accused them of being leaders of violent groups.[^cite_note-380] On 1 August, Machado published a letter in *The Wall Street Journal*, stating that she had gone in to hiding "fearing for my life, my freedom".[^cite_note-GotTrounced-381] In a 19 August interview, [Tarek Saab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tarek_William_Saab), suggested that opposition leaders could be charged as intellectual authors of the deaths that occurred in post-electoral violence.[^cite_note-MHCouldCharge-113]

[Freddy Superlano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freddy_Superlano), a former candidate, was detained by masked men two days after the election,[^cite_note-WSJElectionDatabase-317][^cite_note-BloombergCrackdown-376] and held on unknown charges at [El Helicoide](57062364.md) along with detained journalist [Roland Carreño](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roland_Carre%C3%B1o?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-382] After the election, supporters and representatives of the opposition, as well as journalists, have been detained.[^cite_note-BBCRepressionWorsens-374][^cite_note-383][^cite_note-ChillingVideo-384][^cite_note-VOAonWSJ-231] Opposition leaders said that citizens who witnessed the [vote tally sheets in the electoral process](#Electoral_process) (poll watchers) were persecuted and detained.[^cite_note-ReportProtesters-375]

The press and social media have been subject to censorship.[^cite_note-VOAonWSJ-231][^cite_note-BBC-XBan-385][^cite_note-386] The legislature passed a new law "to more tightly regulate non-governmental organizations, amid criticism of a government crackdown".[^cite_note-RegulateNGO-387]

### Transparency in vote reporting and Supreme Court ruling

Main article: [2024 Venezuelan political crisis § Transparency in vote reporting and Supreme Court ruling](77669383.md#Transparency_in_vote_reporting_and_Supreme_Court_ruling)

Opposition leaders, world leaders and observers urged Maduro to make the vote tallies at the polling station level public,[^cite_note-BBCStayinPower-38] which had not happened as of 21 August.[^cite_note-GuardianPressureGrows-20] Refusing to cede power,[^cite_note-GuardianRefusal-33] Maduro instead asked the [Supreme Tribunal of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Tribunal_of_Justice_\(Venezuela\)) on 1 August to audit and approve the results.[^cite_note-ReutersCourtRatifies-37][^cite_note-BBCStayinPower-38][^cite_note-CriesFoul-39][^cite_note-APCourtCertifies-40]

The BBC stated that the TSJ review is "likely to be conducted behind closed doors" where only members will see the tallies. The [Carter Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carter_Center), anticipating this move, stated that "the TSJ is another government institution, appointed by the government ...  not an independent assessment".[^cite_note-BBCStayinPower-38] *[Efecto Cocuyo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Efecto_Cocuyo)* questioned the independence of the TSJ observers.[^cite_note-EfectoCocuyo_who_TSJ_experts-388]

As anticipated,[^cite_note-BBCStayinPower-38][^cite_note-LoyaltoMaduro-35][^cite_note-HandPicked-36][^cite_note-ElPaisVenezuelaCourt-389] after reviewing materials submitted, the Supreme Justice Tribunal (TSJ), composed of justices loyal to Maduro, validated the CNE's statements of a win by Maduro; the opposition and other observers have characterized their ruling as invalid, stating that the high court has no constitutional authority over elections.[^cite_note-GuardianPressureGrows-20][^cite_note-ReutersCourtRatifies-37][^cite_note-APCourtCertifies-40][^cite_note-LoyaltoMaduro-35] The court stated that the opposition vote tallies were falsified; the Associated Press wrote that the Maduro administration "has claimed—without evidence—that a foreign cyberattack staged by hackers from North Macedonia delayed the vote counting on election night and publication of the disaggregated results."[^cite_note-APCourtCertifies-40]

[NTN24](20800612.md) reported that on 26 August, the CNE webpage was briefly accessible. During the period of access, some people were able to view the names of witnesses of the *actas* and check if they matched what the opposition had reported on *resultadosporvenezuela.com*. According to NTN24, the names were found to match.[^cite_note-CNEOpen-390]

### González arrest warrant and exile

[Caryslia Rodríguez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caryslia_Rodr%C3%ADguez), president of the high court, said that a criminal investigation would be conducted regarding "presumably false" results that were published;[^cite_note-ReutersCourtRatifies-37] González was summoned to appear on accusations, among others, of usurping the CNE's functions by uploading election records to *[www.resultadosconvzla.com](http://www.resultadosconvzla.com/)*;[^cite_note-NTN24FormallySummons-391] he did not appear for the 26 August summons,[^cite_note-ChangeinLife-392][^cite_note-ReutersSecondSummons-393] or the second summons on 27 August.[^cite_note-AFPSecondSummons-394]

After he failed to respond to a third summons, an arrest warrant was issued on 2 September for González for crimes including criminal association and conspiracy after prosecutor Luis Ernesto Dueñez Reyes "requested Gonzalez be arrested for usurpation of functions, falsification of public documents, instigation to disobey the law, conspiracy and association", according to Reuters.[^cite_note-APArrestWarrant-43][^cite_note-GuardianArrestWarrant-395] González sought refuge for several days in the [Spanish embassy in Caracas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_Spain,_Caracas), and was granted asylum, leaving on a Spanish Armed Forces flight for Madrid on 7 September 2024.[^cite_note-ElDiarioAsylum-44][^cite_note-396][^cite_note-ElMundoNegotiatingExile-397]

### Transition proposals

Main article: [2024 Venezuelan political crisis § Transition proposals](77669383.md#Transition_proposals)

Negotiated [peaceful transition of power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peaceful_transition_of_power) proposals include an exit deal[^cite_note-o850-398][^cite_note-wp27jul-399][^cite_note-OfferIncentives-400] and [power-sharing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Power_sharing);[^cite_note-DealSave-401] repeating the election was also proposed.[^cite_note-CriesFoul-39] But Machado said the opposition was willing to negotiate for Maduro to recognize González's win, said the electoral victory was non-negotiable and the opposition would not agree to power-sharing.[^cite_note-OfferIncentives-400] Both the opposition and the Maduro government rejected the idea of repeat elections—both stating they had already won.[^cite_note-AmidCalls-402][^cite_note-RejectCalls-403] Amnesty proposals have been advanced and denied.[^cite_note-NotOffered-404][^cite_note-NoUSAmnesty-405]

Retrieved 30 July 2024. The Venezuelan opposition has moved to show concrete evidence that the election was stolen. ... The opposition said it had collected data from 73% of the country's voting stations on its own and compiled it in a publicly accessible database on Tuesday. Using their national identification, Venezuelans can sign in and review a scanned tally sheet from their voting station showing how many votes went to each candidate. ... 'I found mine, the proof is there,' said Celina Ramirez, an opposition supporter who said she was able to log into the website to locate the tally sheet from her east Caracas voting center, which she said showed González receiving the lion's share of votes. 'There's no way the regime can fool everyone with their tricks,' she added ... The Carter Center, one of the few international organizations invited to monitor the elections, has urged Venezuela's government to release comprehensive polling data at the local level, which is needed to assess the electoral process. ...The opposition hopes the release of the database will increase pressure on Maduro's autocratic regime to make public the detailed results of the election. Also available from [MSN](https://www.msn.com/en-us/news/world/venezuela-s-maduro-moves-to-silence-opposition-after-disputed-election/ar-BB1qTSWi) [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20240730214546/https://www.msn.com/en-us/news/world/venezuela-s-maduro-moves-to-silence-opposition-after-disputed-election/ar-BB1qTSWi) 30 July 2024 at the [Wayback Machine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayback_Machine).
